\Delta^{\prime}\coloneqq(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\circ F(\Delta);\quad\varepsilon^{\prime}\coloneqq\varphi_{0}^{-1}\circ F(\varepsilon);\quad c^{\prime}\coloneqq(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\circ F(c)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}.

Proof.

Since
(C,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is a coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
and since
(F,\varphi_{2}^{-1},\varphi_{0}^{-1})
is an opmonoidal functor
{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
, it follows from
[
12
, Proposition 2.16]
that
(F(C),\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime})
is a coalgebra in
\mathcal{D}
.
On the other hand, since
(C,c)
is a braided object in
\mathcal{C}
and since
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})
is a monoidal functor, it follows from
[
22
, Lemma XIII.3.2]
that
(F(C),c^{\prime})
is a braided object in
\mathcal{D}
.
We prove that the quadruple
(F(C),\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},c^{\prime})
satisfies (
3.4
) and (
3.5
); the other equalities can be proven dually.
We omit the symbol
\circ
.
The equality (
3.4
) is rewritten as

l^{\prime}_{F(C)}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}F(c)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}=r^{\prime}_{F(C)}(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})(F(C)\varotimes F(\varepsilon)).

Since
F(C)\varotimes F(\varepsilon)=(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\varepsilon)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}
by (
2.1
), it suffices to prove that

l^{\prime}_{F(C)}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}F(c)=r^{\prime}_{F(C)}(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\varepsilon),

which using
(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}=(\varphi_{2})_{I,C}^{-1}F(\varepsilon\varotimes C)
(which again follows from (
2.1
)) is rewritten as

l^{\prime}_{F(C)}(\varphi_{0}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{I,C}^{-1}F(\varepsilon\varotimes C)F(c)=r^{\prime}_{F(C)}(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\varepsilon).

Since
l^{\prime}_{F(C)}=F(l_{C})(\varphi_{2})_{I,C}(\varphi_{0}\varotimes F(C))
by (
2.3
) and since
r^{\prime}_{F(C)}=F(r_{C})(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0})
by (
2.4
), this equality is rewritten as
F(l_{C})F(\varepsilon\varotimes C)F(c)=F(r_{C})F(C\varotimes\varepsilon)
, which follows from (
3.4
) for the braided coalgebra
(C,\Delta,\varepsilon,c)
.
On the other hand, the equality (
3.5
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(F(\Delta)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}F(c)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(F(C)\varotimes F(c))(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{{}={}}(F(c)\varotimes F(C))((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))a_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(F(C)\varotimes F(\Delta)).

Since
F(C)\varotimes F(\Delta)=(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\Delta)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}
by (
2.1
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(F(\Delta)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}F(c)

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(F(C)\varotimes F(c))(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{{}={}}(F(c)\varotimes F(C))((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))a_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\Delta),

which using
(F(\Delta)\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}=(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}F(\Delta\varotimes C)
,
F(c)\varotimes F(C)=(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}F(c\varotimes C)(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}
, and
F(C)\varotimes F(c)=(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes c)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}
(which again follow from (
2.1
)), is rewritten as

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}F(\Delta\varotimes C)F(c)

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes c)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{{}={}}a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}F(c\varotimes C)(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}

\displaystyle\qquad\phantom{{}={}}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))a_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\Delta).

On the other hand, since (
2.2
) implies that

a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(a_{C,C,C}),

it suffices to prove that

(3.11)

\displaystyle F(a_{C,C,C})F(\Delta\varotimes C)F(c)

\displaystyle=F(C\varotimes c)(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}

\displaystyle((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}F(c\varotimes C)(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}

\displaystyle((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))a_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\Delta).

Using (
2.2
) again, we can deduce the following equalities:

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}

\displaystyle=F(a_{C,C,C})(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C));

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))a_{F(C),F(C),F(C)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

\displaystyle=F(a_{C,C,C})^{-1}(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}).

By substituting them, the right hand side of (
3.11
) is simplified as

\displaystyle F(C\varotimes c)F(a_{C,C,C})(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(C))((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\varotimes F(C))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,C}^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad F(c\varotimes C)F(a_{C,C,C})^{-1}(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C})(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes C}^{-1}F(C\varotimes\Delta).

Therefore, (
3.11
) is rewritten as

F(a_{C,C,C})F(\Delta\varotimes C)F(c)=F(C\varotimes c)F(a_{C,C,C})F(c\varotimes C)F(a_{C,C,C}^{-1})F(C\varotimes\Delta)
, which follows from (
3.5
) for the braided coalgebra
(C,\Delta,\varepsilon,c)
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal category.
The (large) category
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
of endofunctors of
\mathcal{C}
admits a strict monoidal structure
(\mathord{\varotimes},\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}})
such that
F\varotimes G\coloneqq F\circ G
and
\eta\varotimes\theta\coloneqq\eta\star\theta
for all functors
F,F^{\prime},G,G^{\prime}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
and all natural transformations
\eta\colon{F}\to{G}
and
\theta\colon{F^{\prime}}\to{G^{\prime}}
.
The assignment
X\mapsto X\varotimes(-)
induces a monoidal functor
(\imath,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
, where
(\varphi_{2})_{X,Y}=a_{X,Y,-}^{-1}
for all
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
and
\varphi_{0}=l^{-1}
(see
[
12
, Proposition 1.28]
).
Combining Propositions
3.10
and
3.12
in this situation, we obtain a (strict) monoidal augmented braided simplicial object from a braided coalgebra in a possibly non-strict monoidal category
\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal category, and
(C,c)=(C,\Delta,\varepsilon,c)
a braided coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
.
Then, there exists a unique strict monoidal augmented braided simplicial object
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
in
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
which satisfies the following equalities
:

(3.12)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{1}(C,c)

\displaystyle=C\varotimes(-);

\displaystyle\qquad{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(C,c)(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=l\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(C,c)(\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=a_{C,C,-}\circ(\Delta\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle\qquad{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(C,c)(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})

\displaystyle=a_{C,C,-}\circ(c\varotimes(-))\circ a_{C,C,-}^{-1}.

Proof.

Applying Proposition
3.12
to the monoidal functor
\imath\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
gives rise to a braided coalgebra
(\imath(C),\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},c^{\prime})
in
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
, where
\imath(C)=C\varotimes(-)
, and the morphisms
\Delta^{\prime}
,
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, and
c^{\prime}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\circ\imath(\Delta)=a_{C,C,-}\circ(\Delta\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{0}^{-1}\circ\imath(\varepsilon)=l\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle c^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}^{-1}\circ\imath(c)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}=a_{C,C,-}\circ(c\varotimes(-))\circ a_{C,C,-}^{-1}.

Using Proposition
3.10
, we can construct
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
from this braided coalgebra, as desired.
∎

Remark 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal category, and
(C,c)
a braided coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

When
\mathcal{C}
is strict, Proposition
3.13
yields nothing new.
Indeed, the monoidal functor
\imath\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
is strict in this case, and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
is just the composition
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{X_{\bullet}}\mathcal{C}\overset{\imath}{\longrightarrow}[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
, where
X_{\bullet}
is constructed from
(C,c)
as in Proposition
3.10
.

(2)

The functor
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)\colon{(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
factors through the evident functor
{(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}}\to{(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{S})_{+}^{\mathrm{op}}}
of Remark
2.9

(2)
if and only if
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}(C,c)(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})^{2}=\mathrm{id}
if and only if
c^{2}=\mathrm{id}
.

3.2.
Braided cochain complex and braided cohomology

In this subsection, we define the braided cochain complex and the braided cohomology of a braided coalgebra in a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category, using the associated strict monoidal augmented braided simplicial object constructed in Proposition
3.13
.

Notation 3.15
.

We let
\mathcal{V}
denote the category of
\Bbbk
-modules.

Definition 3.16
.

An
augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
is a functor
{(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
.

Notation 3.17
.

An augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
is typically denoted by
K^{\bullet}
or
K
.
In these cases, we write
K^{n}
for its value on
\langle n\rangle
for all
n\geq 0
.

Let
K^{\bullet}
be an augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
.
The collection
\{K^{n+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
of objects in
\mathcal{V}
gives rise to a cochain complex in
\mathcal{V}
by letting the differential
\delta_{\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n\vphantom{nm}}\colon{K^{n+1}}\to{K^{n+2}}
be the alternating sum
\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n+1\vphantom{nm}})
for all
n\geq 0
.
We will write
K^{\bullet+1}
for this cochain complex in
\mathcal{V}
.
We refer to Lurie
[
24
,
Construction 00QA
and
Remark 04RW
]
for a dual construction.

Definition 3.18
.

Let
K^{\bullet}
be an augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
.
For all
n\geq 0
, we define a
\Bbbk
-submodule
\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
of
K^{n+1}
by
\mathrm{KB}^{0}(K)\coloneqq K^{1}
and

\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)\coloneqq\{\varphi\in K^{n+1}\mid K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi)=-\varphi\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\}\quad\text{for all $n>0$.}

Remark 3.19
.

Let
K^{\bullet}
be an augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
, and
n\geq 0
a fixed integer.
We can define a representation of the braid group
B_{n+1}
on the
\Bbbk
-module
K^{n+1}
as the composition

B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\rho_{n+1}}\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.22083pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}}(\langle n+1\rangle)\overset{K^{\bullet}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{V}}(K^{n+1}),

where
\rho_{n+1}
is defined as in Remark
2.10

(2)
.
Let
\mathrm{sgn}\colon{B_{n+1}}\to{\Bbbk^{\times}}
denote the unique group representation such that
\mathrm{sgn}(g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})=-1
for all
1\leq i\leq n
; said differently,
\mathrm{sgn}
is just the composition of the ordinary sign representation of the symmetric group
{S}_{n+1}
and the quotient map
{B_{n+1}}\to{{S}_{n+1}}
.
By considering their tensor product, we see that
B_{n+1}
acts on the
\Bbbk
-module
K^{n+1}
as
(g,\varphi)\mapsto g{\hskip 0.50003pt}\varphi\coloneqq\mathrm{sgn}(g)(K\circ\rho_{n+1})(g)(\varphi)
.
We claim that
\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
coincides with the
\Bbbk
-submodule
(K^{n+1})^{B_{n+1}}
of
B_{n+1}
-invariants:

(3.13)

\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)=(K^{n+1})^{B_{n+1}}\coloneqq\{\varphi\in K^{n+1}\mid g{\hskip 0.50003pt}\varphi=\varphi\text{ for all }g\in B_{n+1}\}.

Since the group
B_{n+1}
is generated by
\{g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
, it follows that

(K^{n+1})^{B_{n+1}}=\{\varphi\in K^{n+1}\mid g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi=\varphi\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\}.

The desired equality (
3.13
) follows since
g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi=\mathrm{sgn}(g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(K\circ\rho_{n+1})(g_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi)=-K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi)
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
K^{\bullet}
be an augmented braided cosemisimplicial object in
\mathcal{V}
.
Then, the collection
\{\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)\}_{n\geq 0}
of
\Bbbk
-modules determines a subcomplex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(K)
of
K^{\bullet+1}
.

Proof.

Take
n>0
and
\varphi\in\mathrm{KB}^{n-1}(K)
.
For all
1\leq i\leq n
, we can compute

\displaystyle K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\delta_{\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}(\varphi))=K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})\biggl(\,\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)\biggr)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^{j}K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\circ\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j<i-1}(-1)^{j}K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\circ\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+(-1)^{i-1}K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\circ\delta_{i-1\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+(-1)^{i}K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\circ\delta_{i\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+\sum_{j>i}(-1)^{j}K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\circ\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j<i-1}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}}\circ\tau_{i-1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n-1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi)+(-1)^{i-1}K(\delta_{i\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+(-1)^{i}K(\delta_{i-1\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+\sum_{j>i}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}}\circ\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n-1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j<i-1}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(-\varphi)+(-1)^{i-1}K(\delta_{i\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+(-1)^{i}K(\delta_{i-1\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)+\sum_{j>i}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(-\varphi)

\displaystyle\quad=-\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^{j}K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})(\varphi)=-\delta_{\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}(\varphi),

where we used condition
(7)
of Definition
2.6
in the fourth equality, and the fifth equality follows from the assumption.
This implies
\delta_{\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}(\varphi)\in\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
.
Consequently, we have
\delta_{\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}(\mathrm{KB}^{n-1}(K))\subseteq\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
.
∎

This construction
K^{\bullet}\mapsto\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(K)
is functorial in
K
; the next result is sufficient for our purposes.
Given a pair of augmented braided cosemisimplicial objects
K^{\bullet}
and
L^{\bullet}
in
\mathcal{V}
, a natural transformation
{K^{\bullet}}\to{L^{\bullet}}
is denoted by
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet}
, and we write
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn}
for its component
{K^{n}}\to{L^{n}}
at
\langle n\rangle
for all
n\geq 0
.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
K^{\bullet}
and
L^{\bullet}
be augmented braided cosemisimplicial objects in
\mathcal{V}
, and
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet}\colon{K^{\bullet}}\to{L^{\bullet}}
a natural transformation.
Then, the collection
\{f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}\colon{K^{n+1}}\to{L^{n+1}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of
\Bbbk
-linear maps induces a chain map
{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(K)}\to{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(L)}
, which is an isomorphism if
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet}
is a natural isomorphism.

Proof.

We first claim that the collection
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet+1}=\{f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
determines a chain map
{K^{\bullet+1}}\to{L^{\bullet+1}}
.
Write
\delta_{K\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}\bullet\vphantom{nm}}
, and
\delta_{L\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}\bullet\vphantom{nm}}
for the differentials of
K^{\bullet+1}
, and
L^{\bullet+1}
, respectively.
It follows from the naturality of
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet}
that

f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}\circ\delta_{K\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}=\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^{j}(f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}\circ K(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}}))=\sum_{j=0}^{n}(-1)^{j}(L(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})\circ f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn})=\delta_{L\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\vphantom{\prime}n-1\vphantom{nm}}\circ f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn}

for all
n>0
, as desired.
Next, we claim that
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K))\subseteq\mathrm{KB}^{n}(L)
for all
n\geq 0
.
Since
\mathrm{KB}^{0}(K)=K^{1}
and
\mathrm{KB}^{0}(L)=L^{1}
by definition, we may assume that
n>0
.
For all
\varphi\in\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
and
1\leq i\leq n
, it follows that
L(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\varphi))=f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi))=-f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\varphi)
, where the first equality follows from the naturality of
f^{\hskip 0.24582pt\bullet}
, and the second equality from the assumption, and thus
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\varphi)\in\mathrm{KB}^{n}(L)
.
We therefore obtain a chain map
{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(K)}\to{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(L)}
.
Now, suppose that
F
is fully faithful, and take
n\geq 0
.
Since
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}
is an isomorphism of
\Bbbk
-modules, its restriction
{\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)}\to{\mathrm{KB}^{n}(L)}
is injective.
On the other hand, for all
\psi\in\mathrm{KB}^{n}(L)
, the surjectivity of
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}
implies that
\psi=f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\varphi)
for some
\varphi\in K^{n+1}
, and a similar computation gives
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(K(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi))=L(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(\varphi))=L(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\psi)=-\psi=f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}(-\varphi)
for all
1\leq i\leq n
, and thus
\varphi\in\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)
by the injectivity of
f^{\hskip 0.24582ptn+1}
.
It follows that the
\Bbbk
-linear map
{\mathrm{KB}^{n}(K)}\to{\mathrm{KB}^{n}(L)}
is surjective as well.
Hence, the chain map
{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(K)}\to{\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(L)}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Definition 3.22
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category,
(C,c)
a braided coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
, and
M
an object in
\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

We define an augmented braided simplicial object
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
in
\mathcal{C}
to be
\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
.

(2)

We define an augmented braided cosemisimplicial object
\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
in
\mathcal{V}
to be the composite

(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}\overset{\iota}{\longrightarrow}(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}\xrightarrow{{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)}\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.57367pt\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{{\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.36807pt}\mathcal{C}}(-,M)}\mathcal{V}.

(3)

We define the
braided cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
of
(C,c)
with coefficients in
M
to be the cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(\mathrm{K}(C,c,M))
in
\mathcal{V}
, constructed from
\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
as in Definition
3.18
.

(4)

We write
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
for the cohomology of the cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
, and refer to it as the
braided cohomology of
(C,c)
with coefficients in
M
.

We now describe the augmented braided simplicial object
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
explicitly.

Remark 3.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category, and
(C,c)
a braided coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
.
For simplicity, we write
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\coloneqq{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
.
We note that
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{0}(C,c)=\mathcal{X}_{0}(I)=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}(I)=I
and for all
n>0
,

{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{n}(C,c)=\mathcal{X}_{n}(I)=\mathcal{X}(\langle 1\rangle^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}})(I)=((\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\circ{\hskip 0.40974pt}n})(I)=C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I,

which is isomorphic to
C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}
via
C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C}
.
We identify
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
with the augmented braided simplicial object in
\mathcal{C}
determined from the collection
\{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
, together with the collections

\{d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\colon{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\to{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}}\}_{0\leq j\leq n};\quad\{s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\colon{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\to{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+2)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\}_{0\leq j\leq n};\quad\{t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\colon{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\to{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}

of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
, which are defined as follows:

•

The morphism
d^{\hskip 0.81949pt0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{0\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is defined to be the composite
C\xrightarrow{r_{C}^{-1}}C\varotimes I\xrightarrow{\mathcal{X}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.36807pt}0\vphantom{nm}})_{I}}I
.

•

For all
n>0
and
0\leq j\leq n
, the morphism
d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is defined to be the composite

C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C})^{-1}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{\mathcal{X}(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{nm}})_{I}}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C}}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}.

•

For all
0\leq j\leq n
, the morphism
s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is defined to be the composite

C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C})^{-1}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{\mathcal{X}(\sigma_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{n\vphantom{nm}})_{I}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+2)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+2)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}.

•

For all
1\leq i\leq n
, the morphism
t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is defined to be the composite

C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C})^{-1}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{\mathcal{X}(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})_{I}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C}}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}.

We now simplify the above description of the morphism
d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
.
First, the equality
d^{\hskip 0.81949pt0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{0\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}=\varepsilon
holds since

(3.14)

d^{\hskip 0.81949pt0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{0\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}=\mathcal{X}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})_{I}\circ r_{C}^{-1}=l_{I}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes I)\circ r_{C}^{-1}=l_{I}\circ r_{I}^{-1}\circ\varepsilon=\varepsilon,

where the third equality follows from the naturality of
r
, and the fourth equality from
[
22
, Lemma XI.2.3]
.
On the other hand,
d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is equal to the composite

C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes l_{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}

for all
0\leq j<n
, and
d^{\hskip 0.81949ptn\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
is equal to the composite

C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n+1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\varepsilon}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I\xrightarrow{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C}}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}

for all
n>0
.
In order to prove these assertions, since

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}})_{I}

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\circ{\hskip 0.40974pt}j}\star\mathcal{X}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})\star(\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\circ{\hskip 0.40974pt}(n-j)})_{I}

\displaystyle=((\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\circ{\hskip 0.40974pt}j}\star(l\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes(-)))\star(\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\circ{\hskip 0.40974pt}(n-j)})_{I}=(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes l_{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes I)

for all
0\leq j\leq n
, it suffices to prove the equalities

(3.15)

\displaystyle(C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})\circ l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I)

\displaystyle=l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C});

(3.16)

\displaystyle(C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes l_{I})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\varepsilon\varotimes I)

\displaystyle=(C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\varepsilon)\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C})

for all
n>0
.
By the naturality of
l
, (
3.15
) is verified as

\displaystyle(C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})\circ l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I)

\displaystyle=l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}}\circ(I\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes I)

\displaystyle=l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes r_{C})=l_{C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.36807pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}})\circ(C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes r_{C}).

On the other hand, (
3.16
) is reduced to the equality
l_{I}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes I)=\varepsilon\circ r_{C}
, which can be verified similarly to (
3.14
).
The description of the morphism
d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
can be summarized as follows:

(3.17)

d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}=\begin{cases}\varepsilon&if $0=j=n$,\\
C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes(l_{C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}))&if $0\leq j<n$,\\
C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes(r_{C}\circ(C\varotimes\varepsilon))&if $0<j=n$.\end{cases}

The following formulas for the morphisms
s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
and
t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
can be deduced by parallel discussions:

(3.18)

\displaystyle s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}j\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes(a_{C,C,C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\circ(\Delta\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-j)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}))&if $0\leq j<n$,\\
C^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{ijmn}}\varotimes\Delta&if $0\leq j=n$;\end{cases}

(3.19)

\displaystyle t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(i-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes(a_{C,C,C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-i)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}\circ(c\varotimes C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-i)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}})\circ a_{C,C,C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.22083pt(n-i)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}}^{-1})&if $1\leq i<n$,\\
C^{\varotimes\hskip 0.24582pt(n-1)\vphantom{ijmn-+12}}\varotimes c&if $1\leq i=n$.\end{cases}

This implies that
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
coincides with
X_{\bullet}
, constructed as in Proposition
3.10
, when
\mathcal{C}
is strict.

We close this subsection with by relating the above construction to symmetric groups in a special case.

Remark 3.24
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category,
(C,c)
a braided coalgebra in
\mathcal{C}
, and
M
an object in
\mathcal{C}
, and suppose that
c^{2}=\mathrm{id}
.
Then, similarly to Remark
3.14

(2)
, the functor
\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)\colon{(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
factors through the evident functor
{(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}}\to{(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{S})_{+}}
, where
(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{S})_{+}
is defined as in Remark
2.9

(4)
, and thereby induces a representation
{{S}_{n+1}}\to{\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{K}^{n+1}(C,c,M))}
of
{S}_{n+1}
which makes the diagram

\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{K}^{n+1}(C,c,M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{{S}_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{K}^{n+1}(C,c,M))}

commutative for all
n\geq 0
.
Arguing as in Remark
3.19
,
\mathrm{KB}^{n}(C,c,M)
can be regarded as the
\Bbbk
-submodule
\mathrm{K}^{n}(C,c,M)^{{S}_{n+1}}
of
{S}_{n+1}
-invariants.
In this case, the cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
and its cohomology
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
are denoted by
\mathrm{K{S}}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
and
\mathrm{H{S}}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)
, which we refer to as the
symmetric cochain complex
and the
symmetric cohomology of
(C,c)
with coefficients in
M
, respectively.

4.
Braided Morita invariance of braided cochain complex

Let
A
and
B
be
\Bbbk
-algebras.
We write
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}
for the
\Bbbk
-linear category of (left)
A
-modules and of
B
-modules (see Notation
4.17

(1)
).
Two
\Bbbk
-algebras
A
and
B
are said to be
Morita equivalent
if there exists a
\Bbbk
-linear equivalence
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}}
.
When
A
and
B
are endowed with the structure of a
\Bbbk
-bialgebra (see Definition
4.18
),
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
and
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}
admit monoidal structures, and the
\Bbbk
-bialgebras
A
and
B
are said to be
monoidally Morita equivalent
(this terminology is borrowed from
[
37
, Definition 2.3]
) if there exists a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal equivalence
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}}
, which is defined as follows:

Definition 4.1

(cf.
[
12
, Definition 1.30]
)
.

A
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is said to be a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal equivalence
if there exist a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal functor
G\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
as well as
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal natural isomorphisms
{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}}\to{G\circ F}
and
{F\circ G}\to{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{D}}}
.

A
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence
between
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal categories is defined in a similar way.
Two quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-algebras
(A,R)
and
(B,R^{\prime})
are said to be
braided Morita equivalent
(see
[
26
, Definition 1.1]
) if there exists a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence
{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
(for notation, see Subsection
4.3
).
In this section, we prove that the braided cochain complex of quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebras (in the sense of Definition
4.21
) is stable under certain
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalences (see Theorem
4.24
and Corollary
4.25
for details).
We view this result as the (conditional) braided Morita invariance of the braided cochain complex of quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-algebras.

We start with an observation in the general setting: Let
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal equivalence,
(C,c)
and
(D,c^{\prime})
braided coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, and
M
an object in
\mathcal{C}
.
We examine here the following condition:

(
*
)

There exists an isomorphism
\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)\cong\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime},F(M))
for all
M\in\mathcal{C}
.

By Lemma
3.21
, this implies that
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)\cong\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime},F(M))
as cochain complexes of
\Bbbk
-modules, and taking cohomology yields that
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)\cong\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime},M)
as graded
\Bbbk
-modules, for all
M\in\mathcal{C}
.
Let us put
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\coloneqq{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)\circ\iota
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\coloneqq{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime})\circ\iota
.
Take
M\in\mathcal{C}
arbitrarily.
Since
F
is fully faithful by the remark preceding
[
34
, Definition 3.5.13]
,
F
induces an isomorphism
{\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{C}}(-,M)\cong{\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{D}}(F(-),F(M))
by Lemma
2.14
, and pre-whiskering (see
[
20
, Definition 2.1.16]
) with
\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
gives an isomorphism

\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(C,c,M)={\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime},M)\cong{\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{D}}(F\circ\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime},F(M)).

Since
\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime},F(M))={\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},F(M))
by definition, condition (
*
) holds if there is an isomorphism
\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cong F\circ\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
By Lemma
2.13

(1)
, we have
F\circ\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}=\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ[\mathcal{C},F]\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
On the other hand, using
I\cong F(I)
and Lemma
2.13

(2)
, we have
\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cong\mathrm{ev}_{F(I)}\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}=\mathrm{ev}_{I}\circ[F,\mathcal{D}]\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
.
It thus follows that condition (
*
) holds if there is an isomorphism
[F,\mathcal{D}]\circ\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\cong[\mathcal{C},F]\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet}
, or equivalently, if there is a collection
\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural isomorphisms which satisfies (
4.1
) below for all morphisms
\phi
in
(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}
.
We will study natural transformations
{[F,\mathcal{D}]\circ{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime})}\to{[\mathcal{C},F]\circ{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)}
by imposing some compatibility with respect to the (fixed) monoidal structures on
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
, and
F
.

4.1.
Relative morphisms between strict monoidal functors

In this subsection, we let
\mathcal{I}
denote either
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
or
(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}
, which we regard as a strict monoidal category, equipped with the monoidal structure described as in Definitions
2.6
and
2.7
.
We introduce here the notion of relative morphisms between strict monoidal augmented braided (semi)simplicial objects in strict monoidal categories of the form
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
for some category
\mathcal{C}
.
Then, we describe relative morphisms in terms of the evaluations of these functors at the morphisms
\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}}
,
\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}}
, and
\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
in the case
\mathcal{I}=(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
(see Proposition
4.10
).
We also obtain an intermediate result (see Proposition
4.7
), which deals with morphisms satisfying only (
4.2
) and (
4.3
).

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be categories, and
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]}
strict monoidal functors; that is,
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
are strict monoidal augmented braided (semi)simplicial objects in
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
and
[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]
, respectively.
A
relative morphism
from
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
to
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
is a pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
consisting of a functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
and a collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural transformations, which satisfies the equalities

(4.1)

\displaystyle\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}};

(4.2)

\displaystyle\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}_{F};

(4.3)

\displaystyle\eta_{m+n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}

\displaystyle=(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}),

where
m,n\geq 0
and
\phi\colon{\langle m\rangle}\to{\langle n\rangle}
is an arbitrary morphism in
\mathcal{I}
.

Definition
4.2
can be described in the language of
2
-category theory.
We refer to
[
20
]
for the definitions of some terminology which appear in the next remark.

Remark 4.3
.

The strict monoidal category
\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}
gives rise to a
2
-category
\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt(\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}})
with a single object
*
where the
\mathrm{Hom}
-category of
1
-cells from
*
to
*
is equal to
\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}
(see
[
20
, Examples 2.1.19 and 2.3.11]
).
We let
\mathsf{CAT}
denote the (huge)
2
-category of categories.
Given a category
\mathcal{E}
, it follows from
[
24
,
Remark 00F8
]
that there exists a bijective correspondence between strict monoidal functors
{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}]}
and
2
-functors
{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt(\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}})}\to{\mathsf{CAT}}
which sends
*
to
\mathcal{E}
.
Using the notation of Definition
4.2
, if we write
\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{X}
and
\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{Y}
for the
2
-functors
{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt(\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}})}\to{\mathsf{CAT}}
that correspond to
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
, respectively, then, giving a relative morphism
{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
is equivalent to giving a
lax transformation

{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{Y}}
in the sense of
[
20
, Definition 4.2.1]
.
The invertibility condition we wish to impose on a relative morphism
{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
to deduce condition (
*
) can be rephrased by saying that it corresponds to a
strong transformation

{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathsf{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{Y}}
.

Our terminology is justified by the following description in the absolute case:

Remark 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal category,
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
strict monoidal functors.
Then, for any collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}}\to{\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural transformations, the pair
(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}},\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism
{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
if and only if
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
is a monoidal natural transformation
{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}
.
Indeed, (
4.1
) corresponds to the naturality of
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
, and (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) to the extra conditions in Definition
2.2
.

In the rest of this subsection, we prove that the collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
that appears in a relative morphism
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\colon{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
between strict monoidal augmented braided simplicial objects can be recovered from
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
subject to some conditions, using the description of the category
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
in Definition
2.6
.

In what follows, we fix

•

categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
,

•

strict monoidal functors
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]}
, and

•

a functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
.

We first describe a condition on a natural transformation
\theta\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}}
such that
F
lifts to the unique relative morphism
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\colon{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
with the property that
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\theta
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
be a collection of natural transformations.
We define
S_{\eta}
to be the subset of
\mathbb{N}^{2}
consisting of those pairs
(m,n)
such that
(
4.3
)
holds.
Then, for all
\ell,m,n\geq 0
such that
(\ell,m),(m,n)\in S_{\eta}
, the conditions
(\ell+m,n)\in S_{\eta}
and
(\ell,m+n)\in S_{\eta}
are equivalent.

Proof.

This follows from the equalities

\displaystyle(\eta_{\ell+m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell+m}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=(((\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}))\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star\mathcal{Y}_{m}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=(\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star\mathcal{Y}_{m}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=(\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star((\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})))

\displaystyle=(\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m+n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{\ell}\star\eta_{m+n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}),

where the first and fourth equalities follow from the assumptions
(\ell,m)\in S_{\eta}
and
(m,n)\in S_{\eta}
.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\theta\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}}
be a natural transformation.
We inductively define natural transformations
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}
by
\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\coloneqq\mathrm{id}_{F}
and
\eta_{n+1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\coloneqq(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
for all
n\geq 0
.
Then, the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies the equalities
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\theta
,
(
4.2
)
, and
(
4.3
)
for all
m,n\geq 0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F}
and
\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{0}=\theta\star\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}=\theta
, it follows that
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{0})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})=\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{0}=\theta
, whereas (
4.2
) is clearly satisfied.
It now remains to prove that (
4.3
) holds, or equivalently, that
(m,n)\in S_{\eta}
, for all
m,n\geq 0
, where
S_{\eta}
is defined as in Lemma
4.5
.
We argue by double induction.
For all
n\geq 0
, since
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{D}}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
and
\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n}=\mathrm{id}_{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}
, it follows that
(\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{0}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})=\mathcal{Y}_{0}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
, and the case
m=0
follows.
We fix
m\geq 0
, and suppose that (
4.3
) holds for the fixed
m
and all
n\geq 0
.
By the definition of
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
, we have
(1,p)\in S_{\eta}
for all
p\geq 0
, and in particular,
(1,m),(1,m+n)\in S_{\eta}
.
Since
(m,n)\in S_{\eta}
by the inductive hypothesis, Lemma
4.5
implies that
(m+1,n)\in S_{\eta}
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be categories,
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}\colon{\mathcal{I}^{\hskip 0.40974pt\mathrm{op}}}\to{[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]}
strict monoidal functors, and
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
a functor.
Then, the assignment
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\mapsto\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
induces a bijective correspondence between

•

collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies
(
4.2
)
and
(
4.3
)
for all
m,n\geq 0
, and

•

natural transformations
\theta\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}}
.

Proof.

We first note that this assignment is surjective by Lemma
4.6
.
In order to prove the injectivity, take collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
and
\eta_{\bullet}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
of natural transformations subject to the displayed conditions, and suppose that
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
.
We prove that
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
by induction on
n\geq 0
.
The case
n=0
follows since
\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\mathrm{id}_{F}=\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
by (
4.2
).
If the equality
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
holds, then we can compute

\eta_{n+1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})=(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})=(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}})=\eta_{n+1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}},

where the first and fourth equalities follow from (
4.3
), the second equality from
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\prime\vphantom{\theta}}
, and the third equality from the inductive hypothesis.
This shows that the assignment is also injective, as desired.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
be a collection of natural transformations.
For all
m,n\geq 0
, we define
T_{\eta}(m,n)
to be the set of those morphisms
\phi\colon{\langle m\rangle}\to{\langle n\rangle}
in
\mathcal{I}
such that
(
4.1
)
holds.

(1)

For all
\ell,m,n\geq 0
,
\phi\in T_{\eta}(m,n)
and
\psi\in T_{\eta}(\ell,m)
imply that
\phi\circ\psi\in T_{\eta}(\ell,n)
.

(2)

For all
n\geq 0
, we have
\mathrm{id}_{\langle n\rangle}\in T_{\eta}(n,n)
.

(3)

For all isomorphisms
\phi\colon{\langle m\rangle}\to{\langle n\rangle}
in
\mathcal{I}
which belong to
T_{\eta}(m,n)
, we have
\phi^{-1}\in T_{\eta}(n,m)
.

Suppose further that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies
(
4.3
)
.
Then
:

(4)

For all
\phi\in T_{\eta}(m,n)
and
\phi^{\prime}\in T_{\eta}(m^{\prime},n^{\prime})
, we have
\phi\varotimes\phi^{\prime}\in T_{\eta}(m+m^{\prime},n+n^{\prime})
.

Proof.

(1)

This follows from the equalities

\displaystyle\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi\circ\psi)\star F)

\displaystyle=\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ((\mathcal{Y}(\psi)\circ\mathcal{Y}(\phi))\star F)=\eta_{\ell\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\psi)\star F)\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\psi))\circ\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\psi))\circ(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}

\displaystyle=(F\star(\mathcal{X}(\psi)\circ\mathcal{X}(\phi)))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi\circ\psi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}},

where the third and fourth equalities follow from the assumptions
\psi\in T_{\eta}(\ell,m)
and
\phi\in T_{\eta}(m,n)
.

(2)

This follows since
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\mathrm{id}_{\langle n\rangle})\star F)=\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}=\mathrm{id}_{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\mathrm{id}_{\langle n\rangle}))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
.

(3)

Since
F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi)
is a natural isomorphism, this follows from the equalities

\displaystyle\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{-1})\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))^{-1}\circ(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{-1})\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi)^{-1})\circ(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)^{-1}\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{-1}))\circ(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{-1}))\circ\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F)^{-1}=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{-1}))\circ\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}},

where the fourth equality follows from the assumption
\phi\in T_{\eta}(m,n)
.

(4)

Using (
4.3
), we can deduce the following equalities:

\displaystyle\eta_{m+m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi\varotimes\phi^{\prime})\star F)

\displaystyle=\eta_{m+m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{\prime})\star F)

\displaystyle=(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{m}\star\eta_{m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{\prime})\star F)

\displaystyle=(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star(\eta_{m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{\prime})\star F)));

\displaystyle(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi\varotimes\phi^{\prime}))\circ\eta_{n+n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi)\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ\eta_{n+n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi)\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{n^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=(((F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}).

It therefore suffices to prove the equality

(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star(\eta_{m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{\prime})\star F)))=(((F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}).

This can be verified as

\displaystyle(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star(\eta_{m^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi^{\prime})\star F)))

\displaystyle=(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star((F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}))

\displaystyle=(\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{m^{\prime}})\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=((\eta_{m\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\phi)\star F))\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})

\displaystyle=(((F\star\mathcal{X}(\phi))\circ\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\star\mathcal{X}(\phi^{\prime}))\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{n}\star\eta_{n^{\prime}\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}),

where the first and fourth equalities follow from
\phi^{\prime}\in T_{\eta}(m^{\prime},n^{\prime})
and
\phi\in T_{\eta}(m,n)
.

∎

Remark 4.9
.

Using the notation of Lemma
4.8
, conditions
(1)
and
(2)
are rephrased as the following single condition: Given a collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
in general, we can define a subcategory
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
of
\mathcal{I}
, which has the same objects as
\mathcal{I}
, whereas
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}{\hskip-0.40974pt}\hskip 0.81949pt\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}\hskip-0.81949pt}(\langle m\rangle,\langle n\rangle)\coloneqq T_{\eta}(m,n)
for all
m,n\geq 0
.
The condition
(3)
says that the inclusion functor
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{I}
is
conservative
, or in other words, it
reflects isomorphisms
, in the sense that any morphism in
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
which is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{I}
is necessarily an isomorphism in the subcategory
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
as well.
On the other hand, condition
(4)
says that
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
is a monoidal subcategory of
\mathcal{I}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be categories,
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
strict monoidal augmented braided simplicial objects in
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
and
[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]
, respectively, and
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
a functor.
Then, the correspondence
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\mapsto\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of
Proposition
4.7
restricts to a bijective correspondence between

•

collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism from
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
to
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
, and

•

natural transformations
\theta\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}}
which satisfies the following equalities
:

(4.4)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Y}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}}))\circ\theta;

(4.5)

\displaystyle(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{1})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\theta)\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}})\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}}))\circ\theta;

(4.6)

\displaystyle(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{1})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\theta)\circ(\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})\star F)

\displaystyle=(F\star\mathcal{X}(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}))\circ(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{1})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\theta).

Proof.

Take a collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) for all
m,n\geq 0
, and put
\theta\coloneqq\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
.
Since the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), it follows that
\eta_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\mathrm{id}_{F}
,
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\theta
, and
\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\star\mathcal{X}_{1})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})=(\theta\star\mathcal{X}_{1})\circ(\mathcal{Y}_{1}\star\theta)
, so that (
4.4
), (
4.5
), and (
4.6
) are equivalent to the conditions that
\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}}\in T_{\eta}(0,1)
,
\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}}\in T_{\eta}(2,1)
, and
\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\in T_{\eta}(2,2)
.
In view of Proposition
4.7
, it remains to check that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies (
4.1
) for all morphisms
\phi
in
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
if and only if
\theta
satisfies (
4.4
)–(
4.6
).
We note that the former assertion holds if and only if the equality
T_{\eta}(m,n)=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{I}}(\langle m\rangle,\langle n\rangle)
holds for all
m,n\geq 0
, and the necessity follows readily from this.
We now prove the sufficiency.
Suppose that
\theta
satisfies (
4.4
)–(
4.6
).
Since the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
satisfies (
4.3
) (see Proposition
4.7
), conditions
(1)
–
(4)
of Lemma
4.8
are all satisfied, and in particular, we have the subcategory
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
of
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
, defined as in Remark
4.9
.
It follows from the assumptions that
\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}}
,
\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}}
, and
\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}
are morphisms in
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
.
Using conditions
(2)
and
(4)
twice, it follows that
\delta_{j\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}n\vphantom{nm}}=\langle j\rangle\varotimes\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}}\varotimes\langle n-j\rangle
belongs to
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
for all
0\leq j\leq n
.
A similar argument shows that all generating morphisms of
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
belong to
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
.
Note that condition
(3)
is required to prove that
(\tau_{i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})^{-1}
belongs to
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
We therefore obtain the equality
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{\eta}=(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
in view of Remark
2.11
, which implies the former assertion, as desired.
∎

4.2.
Relative morphisms over monoidal functors

In this subsection, we focus on dealing with relative morphisms in the case of interest.
Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be monoidal categories, and
(C,c)=(C,\Delta,\varepsilon,c)
and
(D,c^{\prime})=(D,\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},c^{\prime})
braided coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, and we write
\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
for the monoidal augmented braided simplicial objects in
[\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}]
and
[\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}]
that are constructed from
(C,c)
and
(D,c^{\prime})
as in Proposition
3.13
.
Using the equalities (
3.12
), the involved morphisms are described explicitly as:

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{1}

\displaystyle=C\varotimes(-);

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{1}

\displaystyle=D\varotimes(-);

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=l\circ(\varepsilon\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}(\delta_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=l^{\prime}\circ(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varotimes(-));

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=a_{C,C,-}\circ(\Delta\varotimes(-));

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}(\sigma_{0\vphantom{ij+1m}}^{0\vphantom{nm}})

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}_{D,D,-}\circ(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes(-));

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})

\displaystyle=a_{C,C,-}\circ(c\varotimes(-))\circ a_{C,C,-}^{-1};\qquad

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}(\tau_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})

\displaystyle=a^{\prime}_{D,D,-}\circ(c^{\prime}\varotimes(-))\circ a_{D,D,-}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}.

Let
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a monoidal functor.
We study in more detail the bijective correspondence of Proposition
4.10
in this setting, and describe a relative morphism
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\colon{\mathcal{X}_{\bullet}}\to{\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}}
using a morphism in
\mathcal{D}
, by deforming the equalities (
4.4
)–(
4.6
) with respect to the monoidal structure on
F
.
Since it is still difficult to work in this generality, we make an assumption on the collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of natural transformations as well.
Note that
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
corresponds to the natural transformation
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
, which in view of (
4.7
) is written as

D\varotimes F(-)=\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F\to F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}=F(C\varotimes(-)).

Postcomposing
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
with
(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}^{-1}
gives rise to a natural transformation from
D\varotimes F(-)
to
F(C)\varotimes F(-)
.
We assume that this natural transformation is obtained by tensoring
\mathrm{id}_{F}
with some morphism
{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
.
More explicitly, we assume that
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
satisfies the following condition:

(
**
)

There exists a morphism
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
such that
\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-))
.

Lemma
2.3
guarantees that such a morphism
\alpha
is uniquely determined as follows (if it exists):

(4.11)

\alpha=r^{\prime}_{F(C)}\circ(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}\circ(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{I}\circ(D\varotimes\varphi_{0})\circ r_{D}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
F=(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a monoidal functor, and
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
a morphism in
\mathcal{D}
.
Let
\theta
denote the natural transformation

\mathcal{Y}_{1}\circ F=D\varotimes F(-)\xrightarrow{\alpha\varotimes F(-)}F(C)\varotimes F(-)\xrightarrow{(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}}F(C\varotimes(-))=F\circ\mathcal{X}_{1}.

(1)

\theta
satisfies the equality
(
4.4
)
if and only if
\alpha
satisfies the equality

(4.12)

\varphi_{0}\circ\varepsilon^{\prime}=F(\varepsilon)\circ\alpha.

(2)

\theta
satisfies the equality
(
4.5
)
if and only if
\alpha
satisfies the equality

(4.13)

(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\circ\Delta^{\prime}=F(\Delta)\circ\alpha.

(3)

\theta
satisfies the equality
(
4.6
)
if and only if
\alpha
satisfies the equality

(4.14)

(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\circ c^{\prime}=F(c)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha).

Proof.

(1)

Using (
4.8
), the equality (
4.4
) is rewritten as

l^{\prime}_{F(-)}\circ(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))=F(l)\circ F(\varepsilon\varotimes(-))\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-)).

Since
F(\varepsilon\varotimes(-))\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}=(\varphi_{2})_{I,-}\circ(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.1
), it is rewritten as

l^{\prime}_{F(-)}\circ(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))=F(l)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{I,-}\circ(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(-))\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-)).

Since
l^{\prime}_{F(-)}=F(l)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{I,-}\circ(\varphi_{0}\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.3
) and since
F(l)\circ(\varphi_{2})_{I,-}
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

(\varphi_{0}\varotimes F(-))\circ(\varepsilon^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))=(F(\varepsilon)\varotimes F(-))\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-)),

and the desired equivalence follows from Lemma
2.3
.

(2)

We omit the symbol
\circ
.
Using (
4.9
), the equality (
4.5
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(\Delta\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}(\alpha\varotimes F(-)),

which using the equalities

\displaystyle(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))

\displaystyle=\alpha\varotimes((\varphi_{2})_{C,-}(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))

\displaystyle=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(\alpha\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))

is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(\alpha\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(\Delta\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}(\alpha\varotimes F(-)).

Since
(\alpha\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}=a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))
by the naturality of
a^{\prime}
, it is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(\Delta\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}(\alpha\varotimes F(-)).

Since
(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}=F(a_{C,C,-})(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.2
) and since
F(a_{C,C,-})
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))=F(\Delta\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}(\alpha\varotimes F(-)).

Since
F(\Delta\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}=(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}(F(\Delta)\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.1
) and since
(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(\Delta^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))=(F(\Delta)\varotimes F(-))(\alpha\varotimes F(-)),

and the desired equivalence follows from Lemma
2.3
.

(3)

We omit the symbol
\circ
.
Using (
4.10
), the equality (
4.6
) is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

\displaystyle\quad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-))),

which using the equalities

(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})=\alpha\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(\alpha\varotimes(F(C)\varotimes F(-)))

is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(\alpha\varotimes(F(C)\varotimes F(-)))(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

\displaystyle\quad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-))).

Since

\displaystyle(\alpha\varotimes(F(C)\varotimes F(-)))(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\alpha\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}=a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-)),

by the naturality of
a^{\prime}
, it is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}
