\displaystyle\quad=F(a_{C,C,-})F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-))).

Since
(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}=F(a_{C,C,-})(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.2
) and since
F(a_{C,C,-})
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

\displaystyle\quad=F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-))).

Since

D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-))=(D\varotimes(\alpha\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,D,F(-)}a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}=a^{\prime}_{D,F(C),F(-)}((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

by the naturality of
a^{\prime}
and since
a_{D,D,F(-)}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\quad=F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{D,F(C),F(-)}((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-)).

Since

\displaystyle(\alpha\varotimes F(C\varotimes(-)))(D\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{D,F(C),F(-)}((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad=(\alpha\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{D,F(C),F(-)}((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})(\alpha\varotimes(F(C)\varotimes F(-)))a^{\prime}_{D,F(C),F(-)}((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes F(C))\varotimes F(-))((D\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad=(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))

by the naturality of
a^{\prime}
, it is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad=F(c\varotimes(-))F(a_{C,C,-}^{-1})(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-)).

Using
(\varphi_{2})_{C,C\varotimes(-)}(F(C)\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{C,-})a^{\prime}_{F(C),F(C),F(-)}=F(a_{C,C,-})(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))
again, it is rewritten as

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=F(c\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-)).

Since
F(c\varotimes(-))(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}=(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}(F(c)\varotimes F(-))
by (
2.1
) and since
(\varphi_{2})_{C\varotimes C,-}
is a natural isomorphism, it is equivalent to the equality

\displaystyle((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-))(c^{\prime}\varotimes F(-))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(F(c)\varotimes F(-))((\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\varotimes F(-))((\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\varotimes F(-)),

and the desired equivalence follows from Lemma
2.3
.

∎

Our main result of Subsection
4.2
can be stated as follows:

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be monoidal categories,
(C,c)
and
(D,c^{\prime})
braided coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, and
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
a monoidal functor.
Then, the assignment

(4.15)

\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\mapsto r^{\prime}_{F(C)}\circ(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}\circ(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{I}\circ(D\varotimes\varphi_{0})\circ r_{D}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}

induces a bijective correspondence between

(i)

collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime})
, and
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
satisfies condition
(
**
)
, and

(iii)

morphisms
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
which satisfies
(
4.12
)
–
(
4.14
)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.10
, the assignment
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\mapsto\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
induces a bijective correspondence between
(i)
and

(ii)

natural transformations
\theta\colon{D\varotimes F(-)}\to{F(C\varotimes(-))}
which satisfies (
4.4
)–(
4.6
) as well as the condition that there exist a morphism
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
such that
\theta=(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-))
,

whereas Lemma
4.11
and the preceding arguments imply that the assignment
\alpha\mapsto(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-))
induces a bijective correspondence between
(iii)
and
(ii)
, whose inverse is written explicitly as

\theta\mapsto r^{\prime}_{F(C)}\circ(F(C)\varotimes\varphi_{0}^{-1})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,I}^{-1}\circ\theta_{I}\circ(D\varotimes\varphi_{0})\circ r_{D}^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1};

see also (
4.11
).
The desired correspondence between
(i)
and
(iii)
is the zigzag of these correspondences.
∎

Remark 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a monoidal category, and
(C,\Delta,\varepsilon,c)
and
(D,\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},c^{\prime})
braided coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
, and consider the strict monoidal functor
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.
Theorem
4.12
, in view of Remark
4.4
, asserts that the correspondence
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\mapsto r_{C}\circ(\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{I}\circ r_{D}^{-1}
induces a bijective correspondence between

•

monoidal natural transformations
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime})}\to{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)}
such that

\eta_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
is written in the form
\alpha\varotimes(-)
for some morphism
\alpha\colon{D}\to{C}
in
\mathcal{C}
, and

•

morphisms
\alpha\colon{D}\to{C}
in
\mathcal{C}
which satisfies the equalities
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon\circ\alpha
,
(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\circ\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta\circ\alpha
, and
(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\circ c^{\prime}=c\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)
; that is,
morphisms
\alpha\colon{(D,c^{\prime})}\to{(C,c)}
of braided coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
in the sense of
[
4
, Definition 2.1 (3)]
.

Remark
4.13
suggests that we refer to morphisms
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in the correspondence of Theorem
4.12
as
relative morphisms of braided coalgebras
from
(D,c^{\prime})
to
(C,c)
(with respect to
F
).

Using the notation of Theorem
4.12
, when the morphisms
c
and
c^{\prime}
of the braided coalgebras
(C,c)
and
(D,c^{\prime})
are
globally defined
in the sense that they come from the braidings on
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
as in Example
3.4

(2)
, respectively, the hypotheses on
\alpha
in Theorem
4.12
can be weakened as follows:

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},c)
and
(\mathcal{D},c^{\prime})
be braided monoidal categories,
C
and
D
coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, and
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{(\mathcal{C},c)}\to{(\mathcal{D},c^{\prime})}
a braided monoidal functor.
Then, the correspondence
(
4.15
)
of
Theorem
4.12
induces a bijective correspondence between

•

collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,{c}_{C,C})
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,{c^{\prime}}_{D,D})
and
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
satisfies condition
(
**
)
, and

•

morphisms
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
which satisfies
(
4.12
)
and
(
4.13
)
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that (
4.14
) is automatically satisfied in this case for any morphism
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
, which can be verified as follows:

\displaystyle(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)\circ{c^{\prime}}_{D,D}

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ{c^{\prime}}_{F(C),F(C)}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha)=F({c}_{C,C})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{C,C}\circ(\alpha\varotimes\alpha),

where the first and second equalities follow from (
2.5
) and (
2.8
), respectively.
∎

By replacing
(\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{B})_{+}
with
(\bm{\Delta}_{\hskip 0.24582pt{\textnormal{inj}}}\mathbf{B})_{+}
, we obtain a result which is similar to Corollary
4.14
:

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},c)
and
(\mathcal{D},c^{\prime})
be braided monoidal categories,
C
and
D
coalgebras in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, and
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{(\mathcal{C},c)}\to{(\mathcal{D},c^{\prime})}
a braided monoidal functor.
Then, the correspondence
(
4.15
)
of
Theorem
4.12
induces a bijective correspondence between

•

collections
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,{c}_{C,C})\circ\iota
to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,{c^{\prime}}_{D,D})\circ\iota
and
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
satisfies condition
(
**
)
, and

•

morphisms
\alpha\colon{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
which satisfies
(
4.12
)
.

Remark 4.16
.

We use the notation of Theorem
4.12
.
Let
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=\{\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}_{n}\circ F}\to{F\circ\mathcal{X}_{n}}\}_{n\geq 0}
be a collection of natural transformations such that the pair
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism
{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(C,c)}\to{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(D,c^{\prime})}
, and we write
\alpha
for the morphism
{D}\to{F(C)}
in
\mathcal{D}
that corresponds to
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
via the bijective correspondence (
4.15
).
Then, it is not difficult to show that
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
is a natural isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
if and only if
\alpha
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}
.
Indeed, the necessity follows directly from (
4.15
).
Suppose that
\alpha
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}
.
Then,
\eta_{1}=(\varphi_{2})_{C,-}\circ(\alpha\varotimes F(-))
is a natural isomorphism.
Using (
4.3
), one can prove by induction that
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
is a natural isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.

4.3.
Application: Quasi-triangular bialgebras

In this subsection, we restrict our attention to the study of
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal categories which arise from quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebras.
We briefly review the basic definitions here, and refer to Etingof–Gelaki–Nikshych–Ostrik
[
16
, Section 8.3]
or Kassel
[
22
, Section VIII.2]
for a detailed account of this theory.
We write
\mathord{\varotimes}\coloneqq\mathord{\varotimes}_{\Bbbk}
and
{\dim}\coloneqq{\dim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}}
.
Recall from Notation
3.15
that
\mathcal{V}
denotes the category of
\Bbbk
-modules, which we regard as a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal category with respect to the usual monoidal structure
(\mathord{\varotimes},\Bbbk)
.

Notation 4.17
.

(1)

Let
A
be a
\Bbbk
-algebra.
We write
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
for the
\Bbbk
-linear category of (left)
A
-modules, and we let
U_{A}
or
U
denote the forgetful functor
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
.

(2)

Let
A
be a finite-dimensional algebra over a field
\Bbbk
.
We write
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
for the full subcategory of
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
consisting of finitely generated
A
-modules.

Definition 4.18

(cf.
[
22
, Definition III.2.2]
)
.

A triple
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is called a
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
if
A
is a
\Bbbk
-algebra and
\Delta\colon{A}\to{A\varotimes A}
and
\varepsilon\colon{A}\to{\Bbbk}
are morphisms of
\Bbbk
-algebras such that
(U(A),\Delta,\varepsilon)
is a coalgebra in
\mathcal{V}
.

Given a
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
, the category
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
of Notation
4.17

(1)
admits a monoidal structure
(\mathord{\varotimes},\Bbbk)
such that the forgetful functor
U\colon{\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}
admits a monoidal structure in the obvious way, where for all
A
-modules
M
and
N
,
M\varotimes N
is regarded as an
A
-module via
a(m\varotimes n)\coloneqq\Delta(a)(m\varotimes n)
for all
a\in A
,
m\in M
, and
n\in N
, and
\Bbbk
is regarded as an
A
-module via
a\lambda\coloneqq\varepsilon(a)\lambda
for all
a\in A
and
\lambda\in\Bbbk
(see
[
22
, Proposition XI.3.1]
or Witherspoon
[
42
, page 186]
, for details).

Definition 4.19

(cf.
[
22
, Definitions VIII.2.1 and VIII.2.2]
)
.

A quadruple
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,R)
is called a
quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
if
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is a
\Bbbk
-bialgebra,
R=R_{1}\varotimes R_{2}
is a unit element in
A\varotimes A
(where the summation symbol is suppressed), such that the equality
(\tau\circ\Delta)(a)=R\Delta(a)R^{-1}
holds for all
a\in A
, and the equalities
(\Delta\varotimes A)(R)=R_{1}\varotimes R_{1}\varotimes(R_{2}R_{2})
and
(A\varotimes\Delta)(R)=(R_{1}R_{1})\varotimes R_{2}\varotimes R_{2}
hold, where
\tau\colon{A\varotimes A}\to{A\varotimes A};a\varotimes a^{\prime}\mapsto a^{\prime}\varotimes a
denotes the flip morphism.
In this case, the element
R\in A\varotimes A
is referred to as a (
universal
)
R-matrix
of
A
.

We will often abbreviate a quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,R)
to
(A,R)
.

Remark 4.20
.

Let
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,R)
be a quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra.
Then, the element
R=R_{1}\varotimes R_{2}
gives a braiding
{c}^{R}
on
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
, where for all
A
-modules
M
and
N
, the isomorphism
{c}^{R}_{M,N}\colon{M\varotimes N}\to{N\varotimes M}
is defined by
{c}^{R}_{M,N}(m\varotimes n)\coloneqq(R_{2}n)\varotimes(R_{1}m)
for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
(see
[
22
, Proposition XV.2.2 (a)]
for a proof).
Since
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is a coalgebra in
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
, it follows that
(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A})=(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,{c}^{R}_{A,A})
is a braided coalgebra in
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
(see Example
3.4

(2)
).

Definition 4.21
.

Let
(A,R)
be a quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra, and
M
an
A
-module.
We write

\displaystyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R)\coloneqq{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A});\quad{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R)\coloneqq{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A});\quad\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\coloneqq\mathrm{K}^{\bullet}(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A},M);

\displaystyle\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\coloneqq\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A},M);\quad\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\coloneqq\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A},M),

where the right hand sides are constructed from the braided coalgebra
(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A})
(and an object
M
) in
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
as in Proposition
3.13
and Definition
3.22

(1)
–
(4)
.
We refer to
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
and
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
as the
braided cochain complex
and the
braided cohomology of
(A,R)
with coefficients in
M
, respectively.

Remark 4.22
.

We use the notation of Definition
4.19
.
Recall that a quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
(A,R)
is said to be
triangular
if
R^{-1}=R_{2}\varotimes R_{1}
(see
[
16
, Definition 8.3.3]
).
In this case, the equality
({c}^{R})^{2}=\mathrm{id}
holds, and given an
A
-module
M
, the cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
and its cohomology
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
are constructed using the representations
{{S}_{n+1}}\to{\mathrm{Aut}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{K}^{n+1}(A,R,M))}
of the symmetric groups (see Remark
3.24
), and are denoted by
\mathrm{K{S}}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
and
\mathrm{H{S}}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
, which we call the
symmetric cochain complex
and the
symmetric cohomology of
(A,R)
with coefficients in
M
, respectively.
In particular, when a
\Bbbk
-bialgebra
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is
cocommutative
in the sense that
\tau\circ\Delta=\Delta
holds (see
[
22
, Definition III.1.1 (a)]
), where
\tau
denotes the flip morphism of Definition
4.19
, the pair
(A,1\varotimes 1)
is a triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra, and the cochain complex
\mathrm{K{S}}^{\bullet}(A,1\varotimes 1,M)
is canonically identified (by evident
{S}_{n+1}
-equivariant isomorphisms) with the homogeneous cochain complex
\mathrm{K{S}}^{\bullet}(A,M)
, defined in
[
36
, page 12]
, which is isomorphic to the cochain complex
\mathrm{K{S}}^{\bullet}(G,M)
of
[
30
, Definition 3.1]
for a group
G
, when
\Bbbk=\mathbb{Z}
and
A
is the group algebra of
G
over
\Bbbk
.
In particular, Proposition
3.20
contains
[
36
, Proposition 3.2]
as a special case.

We now give an explicit description of the augmented braided simplicial object
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R)
, in order to highlight that the braided cochain complex
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
and the braided cohomology
\mathrm{HB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)
in the sense of Definition
4.21
are, up to isomorphism, generalizations of the homogeneous cochain complex and the symmetric cohomology of cocommutative
\Bbbk
-Hopf algebras, defined as in
[
36
]
.

Remark 4.23
.

Let
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,R)
be a quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra.
Using Remark
3.23
,
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R)
can be identified with the augmented braided simplicial object which is determined from the collection
\{A^{\varotimes{\hskip 0.40974pt}n}\}_{n\geq 0}
of
A
-modules, together with the collections
\{d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
,
\{s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}
, and
\{t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
of
A
-linear maps, which are defined by the equalities (
3.17
), (
3.18
), and (
3.19
), which in this case admit more concrete forms as

\displaystyle d^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}(a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(a_{j})(a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{j-1}\varotimes a_{j+1}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n});

\displaystyle s^{\hskip 0.81949ptj\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}(a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n})

\displaystyle=a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{j-1}\varotimes a_{j}^{(1)}\varotimes a_{j}^{(2)}\varotimes a_{j+1}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n};

(4.16)

\displaystyle t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}i\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{n\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}(a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n})

\displaystyle=a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{i-2}\varotimes(R_{2}a_{i})\varotimes(R_{1}a_{i-1})\varotimes a_{i+1}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n},

where we used the Sweedler notation
\Delta(a_{j})\coloneqq a_{j}^{(1)}\varotimes a_{j}^{(2)}
.
In particular, when
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
is cocommutative and
R=1\varotimes 1
, the right hand side of (
4.16
) is simplified as
a_{0}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{i-2}\varotimes a_{i}\varotimes a_{i-1}\varotimes a_{i+1}\varotimes\cdots\varotimes a_{n}
, and this already appears in the right hand side of
[
30
, (3.1.1)]
and that of
[
36
, (3.2)]
.

Our main result asserts that the braided cochain complex of quasi-triangular bialgebras is a braided Morita invariant under some condition:

Theorem 4.24
.

Let
(A,R)=(A,\Delta,\varepsilon,R)
and
(B,R^{\prime})=(B,\Delta^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime},R^{\prime})
be quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebras, and suppose that
(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
is a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence such that
U_{B}\circ F\cong U_{A}
as
\Bbbk
-linear functors.
Then, there exists an isomorphism
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\cong\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(B,R^{\prime},F(M))
for all
A
-modules
M
.
In particular, the braided cohomology of quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebras is invariant under braided Morita equivalences which commute with the forgetful functors up to
\Bbbk
-linear natural isomorphism.

Proof.

Take a
\Bbbk
-linear natural isomorphism
\xi^{\prime}\colon{U_{B}\circ F}\to{U_{A}}
.
It follows from a discussion similar to that after Etingof–Gelaki
[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1]
that there exist an isomorphism
\beta\colon{B}\to{A}
of
\Bbbk
-algebras and a
\Bbbk
-linear natural isomorphism
\xi\colon{F}\to{{\beta}^{*}}
, where
\beta^{*}\colon{\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}}
denotes the pullback functor induced from
\beta
(see
[
22
, Section XI.4, Example 2]
for the definition).
Indeed,
\beta
is given explicitly by
\beta(b)\coloneqq\xi^{\prime}_{A}(b\,\xi^{\prime{\hskip 0.40974pt}-1}_{A}(1))
for all
b\in B
, and
\xi
is the unique
\Bbbk
-linear natural isomorphism such that
U_{B}\star\xi=\xi^{\prime}
.
Note that
\beta
can be regarded as a
B
-linear map
{B}\to{\beta^{*}(A)}
.
If we set
\lambda\coloneqq(\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}\circ\varphi_{0})(1)
, which is a unit in
\Bbbk
since
\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}\circ\varphi_{0}
is an automorphism of
\Bbbk
, then,
(F,\lambda\varphi_{2},\lambda^{-1}\varphi_{0})
is also a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence; this rescaling of
(\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})
is taken from the proof of
[
27
, Theorem 2.2]
.
We now claim that the
B
-linear map
\alpha\coloneqq\xi_{A}^{-1}\circ\beta\colon{B}\to{F(A)}
satisfies (
4.12
) with respect to this monoidal functor.
Since we have
F(\varepsilon)\circ\alpha=F(\varepsilon)\circ\xi_{A}^{-1}\circ\beta=\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}\circ\varepsilon\circ\beta
by the naturality of
\xi
, it suffices to prove that the equality
(\lambda^{-1}\varphi_{0}\circ\varepsilon^{\prime})(b)=(\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}\circ\varepsilon\circ\beta)(b)
holds for all
b\in B
, which can be verified as follows:

\displaystyle(\lambda^{-1}\varphi_{0}\circ\varepsilon^{\prime})(b)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\varphi_{0}(\varepsilon^{\prime}(b){\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\lambda^{-1}\varphi_{0}(b{\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\lambda^{-1}(\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}\circ\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}\circ\varphi_{0})(b{\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\lambda^{-1}\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}(b(\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}\circ\varphi_{0})(1))

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}(\lambda b{\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}(b{\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}(\beta(b){\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}((\varepsilon\circ\beta)(b){\hskip 0.50003pt}1)=(\xi_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}^{-1}\circ\varepsilon\circ\beta)(b).

Since
\alpha
is an isomorphism, it follows from Corollary
4.15
(see also Remark
4.16
) that there is a collection
\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
of natural isomorphisms such that
(F,\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})
is a relative morphism
{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R)\circ\iota}\to{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(B,R^{\prime})\circ\iota}
.
By an argument after Definition
4.1
, this gives the desired isomorphism
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\cong\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(B,R^{\prime},F(M))
.
∎

We now focus on the finite-dimensional case.
Let
\Bbbk
be a field, and
(A,R)
a finite-dimensional quasi-triangular
\Bbbk
-bialgebra.
In this case,
A
is contained in the monoidal full subcategory
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
of
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
, defined as in Notation
4.17

(2)
, and
{c}^{R}
restricts to a braiding on
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
(also denoted
{c}^{R}
), and we thus obtain a braided coalgebra
(A,{c}^{R}_{A,A})
in
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
.

Corollary 4.25
.

Let
(A,R)
and
(B,R^{\prime})
be finite-dimensional quasi-triangular bialgebras over a field
\Bbbk
, and suppose that
F\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
is a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence.
Then, there exists an isomorphism
\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(A,R,M)\cong\mathrm{KB}^{\bullet}(B,R^{\prime},F(M))
for all finitely generated
A
-modules
M
.
In particular, the braided cohomology of finite-dimensional quasi-triangular bialgebras over a field is a braided Morita invariant.

In order to deduce this from Theorem
4.24
, we first need to prove the following result:

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
(A,R)
and
(B,R^{\prime})
be finite-dimensional quasi-triangular bialgebras over a field
\Bbbk
.
Then, any
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal functor
F=(F,\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
extends uniquely, up to isomorphism, to a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal functor
\widetilde{F}=(\widetilde{F},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{0})\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
which commutes with filtered colimits.
If
F
is a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence, then so is
\widetilde{F}
.

The proof of Lemma
4.26
requires us to write modules as filtered colimits of finitely generated ones, in a way that is compatible with tensor products.
To achieve this, we write them as filtered colimits of all finitely generated submodules.
We also need to keep track of relevant isomorphisms explicitly, as treating them as abstract ones is insufficient to show that the induced functor commutes with the braidings.

Remark 4.27
.

Let
A
be a finite-dimensional bialgebra over a field
\Bbbk
.
Then, the
\Bbbk
-linear category
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
is
locally coherent Grothendieck
in the sense of Herzog
[
19
, page 511]
, and
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
coincides with the full subcategory
\mathrm{fp}({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}})
of
finitely presented
objects in
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
, which satisfies the finiteness assumptions of Xu–Zheng
[
43
, page 69]
.
Since the tensor product functor on
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
commutes with filtered colimits, the aforementioned monoidal structure on
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}
coincides (up to isomorphism) with the monoidal structure inherited from that on
{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}
as in
[
43
, Remark 3.4]
.

Notation 4.28
.

Let
A
be a finite-dimensional bialgebra over a field
\Bbbk
, and
M
an
A
-module.

(1)

We write
\mathcal{J}_{M}
for the partially ordered set consisting of finitely generated
A
-submodules of
M
.

We regard
\mathcal{J}_{M}
as a (small) category in the usual way (see
[
25
, page 11]
).
Since
\mathcal{J}_{M}
is directed, it is filtered when regarded as a category, and the collection
\{\iota_{M^{\prime}}\colon M^{\prime}\hookrightarrow M\}_{M^{\prime}\in{\hskip 0.40974pt\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{J}_{M}}
of inclusion maps exhibits
M
as a filtered colimit of the evident functor
J_{M}\colon{\mathcal{J}_{M}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a category, and
G\colon{\mathcal{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
a functor.
We write
\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}M^{\prime}\subseteq M}{\hskip-1.00006ptG(M^{\prime})}
or
\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{J}_{M}}{\hskip-1.00006ptG(M^{\prime})}
for the filtered colimit
\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{J}_{M}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.00006pt(G\circ J_{M})}
of the composition
\mathcal{J}_{M}\xrightarrow{J_{M}}{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}\overset{G}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{D}
, if it exists.

Proof of
Lemma
4.26
.

Applying
[
43
, Lemma 3.6]
, we can extend
F
uniquely, up to isomorphism, to a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal functor
\widetilde{F}\colon{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}}
which commutes with filtered colimits.
Note that the proof of
[
43
, Lemma 3.6]
remains valid when finitely presented objects are not necessarily rigid, and is thus applicable in this case.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that the induced functor
{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\textnormal{fin}}}
is equal to
F
in the strict sense.
We claim that
\widetilde{F}
is a braided monoidal functor
{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R^{\prime}})}
.
For an
A
-module
L
, we let
\{s_{L^{\prime}}\colon{F(L^{\prime})}\to{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}L^{\prime}\subseteq L}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(L^{\prime})}}\}_{L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}}
denote the collection of
B
-linear map forming the (filtered) colimit
\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}L^{\prime}\subseteq L}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(L^{\prime})}
, and we define
\phi_{L}\colon{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}L^{\prime}\subseteq L}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(L^{\prime})}}\to{\widetilde{F}(L)}
to be the unique
B
-linear map such that
\phi_{L}\circ s_{L^{\prime}}=\widetilde{F}(\iota_{L^{\prime}})
for all
L^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{L}
, which is an isomorphism since
\widetilde{F}
commutes with filtered colimits.
Take
A
-modules
M
and
N
.
Observe that the isomorphism
(\varphi_{2})_{M,N}\colon{\widetilde{F}(M)\varotimes\widetilde{F}(N)}\to{\widetilde{F}(M\varotimes N)}
factors as a composition of isomorphisms of
B
-modules as follows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}(M)\varotimes\widetilde{F}(N)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{M}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{N}^{-1}}\Bigl(\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\mathcal{J}_{M}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptF(M^{\prime})}\Bigr)\varotimes\Bigl(\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})}\Bigr)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{M,N}^{-1}}\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-3.99994pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime}))}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.36807pt}}{\hskip-0.81949pt(\varphi_{2})_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}}}\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-3.99994ptF(M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\chi_{M,N}}\displaystyle\varinjlim_{\mathcal{J}_{M\varotimes N}}{\hskip-1.00006pt\hskip-1.99997ptF(X^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\phi_{M\varotimes N}}\widetilde{F}(M\varotimes N).

Here,
\gamma_{M,N}
is defined to be the composition of the dotted isomorphisms below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime}))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime}))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}M^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime}))})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}M^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})}))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}M^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(M^{\prime})})\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{F}(M)\varotimes(\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}N^{\prime}}{\hskip-1.00006ptF(N^{\prime})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes\widetilde{F}(N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{F}(M)\varotimes\widetilde{F}(N),}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M^{\prime})\varotimes s_{N^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M^{\prime})\varotimes s_{N^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{M^{\prime}}\varotimes\varinjlim{F(N^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{M}\varotimes(\varinjlim{F(N^{\prime})})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M^{\prime})\varotimes s_{N^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}(\iota_{M^{\prime}})\varotimes(\varinjlim{F(N^{\prime})})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M^{\prime})\varotimes\phi_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}(M)\varotimes\phi_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(M^{\prime})\varotimes\widetilde{F}(\iota_{N^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}(\iota_{M^{\prime}})\varotimes\widetilde{F}(N)}

and
\chi_{M,N}
is the canonical isomorphism induced from the functor
{\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}\to{\mathcal{J}_{M\varotimes N}};(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\mapsto M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime}
, whose inverse is constructed using the property that for all
X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{M\varotimes N}
, we can choose
M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{M}
and
N^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{N}
so that
X^{\prime}\subseteq M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime}
.
Given a functor
H\colon{\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}\to{{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}}
, we will identify
\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}{\hskip-1.00006ptH}
with the filtered colimit of the composition
\mathcal{J}_{N}\times\mathcal{J}_{M}\to\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}\xrightarrow{H}{\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.883pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.91846pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{B}}}\mathcal{M}}
.
We now wish to prove the equality
(\varphi_{2})_{N,M}\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}(M),\widetilde{F}(N)}=\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{M,N}
(see also (
2.8
)), which is equivalent to the equality

\displaystyle\phi_{N\varotimes M}\circ\chi_{N,M}\circ\varinjlim{(\varphi_{2})_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}}\circ\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{N}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{M}^{-1})\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}(M),\widetilde{F}(N)}\circ(\phi_{M}\varotimes\phi_{N})\circ\gamma_{M,N}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ\phi_{M\varotimes N}\circ\chi_{M,N}\circ\varinjlim{(\varphi_{2})_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}}.

We write
t_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}
for the evident
B
-linear map
{F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime})}\to{\varinjlim_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\mathcal{J}_{M}\times\mathcal{J}_{N}}{\hskip-1.00006pt(F(M^{\prime})\varotimes F(N^{\prime}))}}
.
Since

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ\phi_{M\varotimes N}\circ\chi_{M,N}\circ t_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ\phi_{M\varotimes N}\circ s_{M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ\widetilde{F}(\iota_{M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime}})=\widetilde{F}(\iota_{N^{\prime}\varotimes M^{\prime}})\circ F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\phi_{N\varotimes M}\circ s_{N^{\prime}\varotimes M^{\prime}}\circ F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\phi_{N\varotimes M}\circ\chi_{N,M}\circ t_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}\circ F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\phi_{N\varotimes M}\circ\chi_{N,M}\circ\varinjlim{F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})}\circ t_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}},

where the third equality follows from (
2.5
) for
{c}^{R}
,
\iota_{N^{\prime}\varotimes M^{\prime}}=\iota_{N^{\prime}}\varotimes\iota_{M^{\prime}}
, and
\iota_{M^{\prime}\varotimes N^{\prime}}=\iota_{M^{\prime}}\varotimes\iota_{N^{\prime}}
, it follows that
\widetilde{F}({c}^{R}_{M,N})\circ\phi_{M\varotimes N}\circ\chi_{M,N}=\phi_{N\varotimes M}\circ\chi_{N,M}\circ\varinjlim{F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})}
, and it suffices to prove the equality

(4.17)

\begin{split}&\varinjlim{(\varphi_{2})_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}}\circ\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{N}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{M}^{-1})\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}(M),\widetilde{F}(N)}\circ(\phi_{M}\varotimes\phi_{N})\circ\gamma_{M,N}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\varinjlim{F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})}\circ\varinjlim{(\varphi_{2})_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{N}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{M}^{-1})\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}(M),\widetilde{F}(N)}\circ(\phi_{M}\varotimes\phi_{N})\circ\gamma_{M,N}\circ t_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{N}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{M}^{-1})\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{\widetilde{F}(M),\widetilde{F}(N)}\circ(\widetilde{F}(\iota_{M^{\prime}})\varotimes\widetilde{F}(\iota_{N^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle\quad=\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(\phi_{N}^{-1}\varotimes\phi_{M}^{-1})\circ(\widetilde{F}(\iota_{N^{\prime}})\varotimes\widetilde{F}(\iota_{M^{\prime}}))\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\quad=\gamma_{N,M}^{-1}\circ(s_{N^{\prime}}\varotimes s_{M^{\prime}})\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}=t_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}=\varinjlim{{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}}\circ t_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}},

where the first and the fourth equalities follow from the commutativity of the above diagram, and the second equality from (
2.5
) for
{c}^{R^{\prime}}
.
Thus, the left hand side of (
4.17
) is
\varinjlim{(\varphi_{2})_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}}\circ\varinjlim{{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}}
.
We are reduced to prove the equality
(\varphi_{2})_{N^{\prime},M^{\prime}}\circ{c}^{R^{\prime}}_{F(M^{\prime}),F(N^{\prime})}=F({c}^{R}_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{M^{\prime},N^{\prime}}
for all
M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{M}
and
N^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}_{N}
, which follows since
F
is a braided monoidal functor by virtue of our assumption.
∎

Proof of
Corollary
4.25
.

By Theorem
4.24
and Lemma
4.26
, and also the proof of
[
14
, Proposition 4.2.2]
, it suffices to prove that
F^{-1}(B)\cong A
as
A
-modules.
Let
\{[S_{1}],\dots,[S_{m}]\}
be the set of isomorphism classes of simple
A
-modules, and we write
P_{i}
for the projective cover of
S_{i}
. We note that
\{[P_{1}],\dots,[P_{m}]\}
forms the set of isomorphism classes of finitely generated indecomposable projective
A
-modules, and given a finitely generated projective
A
-module
P
, the multiplicity of
P_{i}
in
P
is
\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(P,S_{i})/\dim{\mathrm{End}}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(S_{i})
; see
[
14
, Theorem 4.3.1 (2)]
for the special case when
P=A
and
\Bbbk
is algebraically closed.
Arguing as in the proof of
[
14
, Proposition 4.2.2]
, we are reduced to prove
\dim F(S_{i})=\dim S_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq m
.
This follows from the proof of Etingof–Gelaki
[
15
, Theorem 6.1]
, using the Frobenius-Perron theorem (cf.
[
16
, Theorem 3.2.1 (3)]
) and the fact that for all
1\leq i,j,j^{\prime}\leq m
, the multiplicity of
F(S_{j})
in the composition series of
F(S_{i})\varotimes F(S_{j^{\prime}})\cong F(S_{i}\varotimes S_{j^{\prime}})
is equal to that of
S_{j}
in the composition series of
S_{i}\varotimes S_{j^{\prime}}
.
The last assertion follows since
F
is a
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal equivalence.
∎

Remark 4.29
.

Replacing
\dim
by the length as a
\Bbbk
-module, Corollary
4.25
and the preceding remark hold in a more general setting where
\Bbbk
is a nonzero commutative artinian ring and
A
and
B
are finitely generated as
\Bbbk
-modules; that is, when
A
and
B
are
artinian
\Bbbk
-algebras
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a field of characteristic different from two.
We now determine the
\Bbbk
-vector space
\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R,M)
up to isomorphism when
A
is the Sweedler Hopf algebra, which is an example of a non-cocommutative
\Bbbk
-Hopf algebra which is not isomorphic to any group algebras.
When
\Bbbk
is an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero,

A
is precisely the
\Bbbk
-Hopf algebra of the smallest dimension having this property.

Example 4.30
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a field of characteristic different from two.
Let
(A,\Delta,\varepsilon)
be the
4
-dimensional Sweedler Hopf algebra, where
A
is generated by
g
and
x
subject to the relations
g^{2}=1
,
x^{2}=0
, and
xg=-gx
, and
\Delta
,
\varepsilon
, and the antipode
S
are determined by
\Delta(g)\coloneqq g\varotimes g
,
\Delta(x)\coloneqq x\varotimes g+1\varotimes x
,
\varepsilon(g)\coloneqq 1
,
\varepsilon(x)\coloneqq 0
,
S(g)\coloneqq g
, and
S(x)\coloneqq gx
.
It follows from Radford
[
33
, pages 296 and 297]
that
(A,R)
is a (quasi-)triangular
\Bbbk
-algebra if and only if
R
is written in the following form for an arbitrary
\lambda\in\Bbbk
:

R=R_{\lambda}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(1\varotimes 1+1\varotimes g+g\varotimes 1-g\varotimes g)+\frac{\lambda}{2}(x\varotimes x+x\varotimes gx-gx\varotimes x+gx\varotimes gx).

Recall from Andruskiewitsch–Etingof–Gelaki
[
1
, Example 3.4.2]
that
J\coloneqq 1\varotimes 1-\dfrac{\lambda}{2}(x\varotimes gx)
is a
bialgebra twist of
A
in the sense of
[
16
, Definition 5.14.1]
, and thus
J
gives rise to a
\Bbbk
-linear braided monoidal equivalence
(\mathrm{id},\varphi_{2},\mathrm{id})\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R_{0}})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R_{\lambda}})}
, where, for all
A
-modules
M
and
N
, the isomorphism
(\varphi_{2})_{M,N}\colon{M\varotimes N}\to{M\varotimes N}
of
A
-modules is defined by
(\varphi_{2})_{M,N}(m\varotimes n)\coloneqq J^{-1}(m\varotimes n)
for all
m\in M
and
n\in N
.
Let
M
be an
A
-module.
By Theorem
4.24
, we know that
\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R_{0},M)\cong\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R_{\lambda},M)
as
\Bbbk
-vector spaces.
We will construct such an isomorphism explicitly in two different ways.
We identify
\mathrm{K}^{2}(A,R_{\lambda},M)=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}({\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{2}(A,R_{\lambda}),M)
with
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)
as in Remark
3.23
.
We note that the braid group
B_{2}
(which is just the infinite cyclic group) acts on
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)
as

\displaystyle(g_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi)(a_{0}\varotimes a_{1})=\mathrm{sgn}(g_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(\varphi\circ t^{{\hskip 0.40974pt}1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}_{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}})(a_{0}\varotimes a_{1})=-\varphi(((R_{\lambda})_{2}a_{1})\varotimes((R_{\lambda})_{1}a_{0}))

for all
a_{0},a_{1}\in A
and all
A
-linear maps
\varphi\colon{A\varotimes A}\to{M}
(see Remark
3.19
and (
4.16
)).
We will further make use of an isomorphism
{\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)}\to{\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}(A,M)};\varphi\mapsto\varphi(1\varotimes(-))
, whose inverse is given by
\phi\mapsto[a_{0}\varotimes a_{1}\mapsto a_{0}^{(1)}\phi(S(a_{0}^{(2)})a_{1})]
, (see
[
42
, page 186]
, for example).

(1)

Take an
A
-linear map
\varphi\colon{A\varotimes A}\to{M}
, and put
\phi\coloneqq\varphi(1\varotimes(-))
.
Since

\displaystyle(g_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi)(1\varotimes a)

\displaystyle=-\varphi(((R_{\lambda})_{2}a)\varotimes(R_{\lambda})_{1})=-((R_{\lambda})_{2}a)^{(1)}\phi(S(((R_{\lambda})_{2}a)^{(2)})(R_{\lambda})_{1})

\displaystyle=-(R_{\lambda})_{2}^{(1)}a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)})S((R_{\lambda})_{2}^{(2)})(R_{\lambda})_{1})

for all
a\in A
,
\varphi
belongs to
\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R_{\lambda},M)
if and only if
g_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi=\varphi
holds if and only if the equality
(R_{\lambda})_{2}^{(1)}a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)})S((R_{\lambda})_{2}^{(2)})(R_{\lambda})_{1})=-\phi(a)
holds for all
a\in A
; the left hand side is written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}((1-g)a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)}))+(1+g)a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)})g))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\lambda}{2}((-x+gx)a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)})x)+(x+gx)a^{(1)}\phi(S(a^{(2)})gx)).

Substituting
a=1
,
g
,
x
, and
gx
into the above equality gives

\displaystyle(1-g)\phi(1)+(1+g)\phi(g)+\lambda((-x+gx)\phi(x)+(x+gx)\phi(gx))

\displaystyle=-2{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(1);

\displaystyle(1+g)\phi(1)+(-1+g)\phi(g)+\lambda((-x-gx)\phi(x)+(-x+gx)\phi(gx))

\displaystyle=-2{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(g);

\displaystyle(x+gx)\phi(1)+(x-gx)\phi(g)+(1+g)\phi(x)+(-1+g)\phi(gx)

\displaystyle=-2{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(x);

\displaystyle(-x+gx)\phi(1)+(x+gx)\phi(g)+(-1+g)\phi(x)+(-1-g)\phi(gx)

\displaystyle=-2{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(gx),

which simultaneously hold if and only if the following equalities are satisfied:

4{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(1)=-(2{\hskip 0.50003pt}(1+g)\phi(g)-\lambda(3+g)x\phi(x));\quad 4{\hskip 0.50003pt}\phi(gx)=-((3-g)x\phi(g)+2{\hskip 0.50003pt}(1-g)\phi(x)).

Since
(1,g,x,gx)
forms a
\Bbbk
-basis of
A
, it follows that the assignment
\phi\mapsto(\phi(1),\phi(g),\phi(x),\phi(gx))
induces an isomorphism
\{\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}(A,M)\mid g_{1\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{1\vphantom{n-NoValue-}}\varphi=\varphi\}\cong M_{\lambda}
, where
M_{\lambda}
is defined to be the
\Bbbk
-subspace of
M^{\oplus 4}
which consists of elements of the form

\Bigl(-\frac{1}{4}(2{\hskip 0.50003pt}(1+g)m_{2}-\lambda(3+g)xm_{3}),m_{2},m_{3},-\frac{1}{4}((3-g)xm_{2}+2{\hskip 0.50003pt}(1-g)m_{3})\Bigr)

for all
m_{2},m_{3}\in M
.
Since
M_{\lambda}\cong M^{\oplus 2}
holds for an arbitrary
\lambda\in\Bbbk
, it follows that

\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R_{0},M)\cong M_{0}\cong M^{\oplus 2}\cong M_{\lambda}\cong\mathrm{KB}^{1}(A,R_{\lambda},M).

(2)

Our second approach uses the proofs of Corollary
4.15
and Theorem
4.24
, as they provide an explicit recipe to construct a relative morphism
{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R_{0})\circ\iota}\to{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R_{\lambda})\circ\iota}
from the
\Bbbk
-linear monoidal equivalence
(\mathrm{id},\varphi_{2},\mathrm{id})\colon{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R_{0}})}\to{({\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.51392pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.68063pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\mathcal{M}}}_{\mathmakebox[0pt][r]{A}}}\mathcal{M}},{c}^{R_{\lambda}})}
mentioned above.
Using the notation of Theorem
4.24
, since
U_{A}\circ\mathrm{id}=U_{A}
as
\Bbbk
-linear functors, we may assume
\xi^{\prime}=\mathrm{id}_{U_{A}}
, and consequently,
\beta
,
\xi
, and
\alpha
become the identities.
We now use Corollary
4.15
(and Remark
4.16
) to obtain from
\alpha
a relative morphism
(\mathrm{id},\eta_{\bullet}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})\colon{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R_{0})\circ\iota}\to{{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{\bullet}(A,R_{\lambda})\circ\iota}
such that
\eta_{n\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}
is a natural isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.
Following the argument after Definition
4.1
applied to this case, precomposing with
(\mathrm{ev}_{\Bbbk}\star\eta)_{2}=(\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{\Bbbk}
gives rise to an isomorphism

\mathrm{K}^{2}(A,R_{0},M)=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}({\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{2}(A,R_{0}),M)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}({\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{2}(A,R_{\lambda}),M)=\mathrm{K}^{2}(A,R_{\lambda},M)

of
\Bbbk
-vector spaces.
Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
3.21
, one can verify that this is compatible with the actions of the braid group
B_{2}
.
Now, we determine the isomorphism
(\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{\Bbbk}
from
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{2}(A,R_{\lambda})
to
{\widetilde{\mathrm{T}}}_{2}(A,R_{0})
, both of which are equal to
A\varotimes(A\varotimes\Bbbk)
.
Using the notation of Theorem
4.12
, the natural transformation
\theta\colon{A\varotimes(-)}\to{A\varotimes(-)}
coincides with
(\varphi_{2})_{A,-}
.
In view of the equalities
{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{1}(A,R_{0})(\Bbbk)=A\varotimes\Bbbk
and
\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}}=(\theta\star{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{1}(A,R_{0}))\circ({\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}}_{1}(A,R_{\lambda})\star\theta)
(the latter follows from the proof of Proposition
4.10
), we have
(\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{\Bbbk}=\theta_{A\varotimes\Bbbk}\circ(A\varotimes\theta_{\Bbbk})=(\varphi_{2})_{A,A\varotimes\Bbbk}\circ(A\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{A,\Bbbk})
.
The composite

A\varotimes A\xrightarrow{(A\varotimes r_{A})^{-1}}A\varotimes(A\varotimes\Bbbk)\xrightarrow{(\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{\Bbbk}}A\varotimes(A\varotimes\Bbbk)\xrightarrow{A\varotimes r_{A}}A\varotimes A

is therefore equal to
(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}
, since

\displaystyle(A\varotimes r_{A})\circ(\eta_{2\vphantom{ij+1-NoValue-}}^{\vphantom{\prime\theta}})_{\Bbbk}\circ(A\varotimes r_{A})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(A\varotimes r_{A})\circ(\varphi_{2})_{A,A\varotimes\Bbbk}\circ(A\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{A,\Bbbk})\circ(A\varotimes r_{A})^{-1}

\displaystyle=r_{A\varotimes A}\circ a_{A,A,\Bbbk}^{-1}\circ(\varphi_{2})_{A,A\varotimes\Bbbk}\circ(A\varotimes(\varphi_{2})_{A,\Bbbk})\circ a_{A,A,\Bbbk}\circ r_{A\varotimes A}^{-1}

\displaystyle=r_{A\varotimes A}\circ(\varphi_{2})_{A\varotimes A,\Bbbk}\circ((\varphi_{2})_{A,A}\varotimes\Bbbk)\circ r_{A\varotimes A}^{-1}

\displaystyle=r_{A\varotimes A}\circ((\varphi_{2})_{A,A}\varotimes\Bbbk)\circ r_{A\varotimes A}^{-1}=(\varphi_{2})_{A,A},

where the second equality follows from
[
22
, Lemma XI.2.2]
, the third and fourth equality from (
2.2
) and (
2.4
), and the fifth equality from the naturality of
r
.
Consequently, the composition

\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)\to\mathrm{K}^{2}(A,R_{0},M)\to\mathrm{K}^{2}(A,R_{\lambda},M)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)

is given by
\varphi\mapsto\varphi\circ(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}
.
We now precompose this with the composition of the isomorphisms

M^{\oplus 4}\to\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}\Bbbk}(A,M)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)

mentioned above, and also postcompose with its inverse
\mathrm{Hom}_{{\hskip 0.40974pt}A}(A\varotimes A,M)\to M^{\oplus 4}
, in order to obtain a
B_{2}
-equivariant isomorphism
{M^{\oplus 4}}\to{M^{\oplus 4}}
, where
B_{2}
acts differently on its domain and codomain. Take an
A
-linear map
\varphi\colon{A\varotimes A}\to{M}
, and put
\phi\coloneqq\varphi(1\varotimes(-))
.
Since we have

(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}(1\varotimes a)=J^{-1}(1\varotimes a)=\Bigl(1\varotimes 1+\frac{\lambda}{2}(x\varotimes gx)\Bigr)(1\varotimes a)

for all
a\in A
, it follows that

(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}(1\varotimes a)=\begin{cases}1\varotimes 1+\dfrac{\lambda}{2}(x\varotimes gx)\\
1\varotimes g-\dfrac{\lambda}{2}(x\varotimes x)\end{cases}=\begin{cases}1\varotimes 1+\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x(1\varotimes x)&if $a=1$,\\
1\varotimes g-\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x(1\varotimes gx)&if $a=g$,\end{cases}

where the second equality follows since we have

a_{0}\varotimes a_{1}=a_{0}^{(1)}\varotimes\varepsilon(a_{0}^{(2)})a_{1}=a_{0}^{(1)}\varotimes a_{0}^{(2)}S(a_{0}^{(3)})a_{1}=a_{0}^{(1)}(1\varotimes S(a_{0}^{(2)})(a_{1}))

for all
a_{0},a_{1}\in A
; for example, one can compute

x\varotimes gx=x(1\varotimes S(g)gx)+g(1\varotimes S(x)gx)=x(1\varotimes g^{2}x)-g(1\varotimes xgx)=x(1\varotimes x).

Since we have
(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}(1\varotimes x)=1\varotimes x
and
(\varphi_{2})_{A,A}(1\varotimes gx)=1\varotimes gx
, it follows that

(\varphi\circ(\varphi_{2})_{A,A})(1\varotimes a)=\begin{cases}\varphi(1\varotimes 1)+\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x\varphi(1\varotimes x)\\
\varphi(1\varotimes g)-\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x\varphi(1\varotimes gx)\\
\varphi(1\varotimes x)\\
\varphi(1\varotimes gx)\end{cases}=\begin{cases}\phi(1)+\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x\phi(x)&if $a=1$,\\
\phi(g)-\dfrac{\lambda}{2}x\phi(gx)&if $a=g$,\\
\phi(x)&if $a=x$,\\
\phi(gx)&if $a=gx$.\end{cases}

Consequently, the
B_{2}
-equivariant isomorphism
{M^{\oplus 4}}\to{M^{\oplus 4}}
is given by

(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3},m_{4})\mapsto\Bigl(m_{1}+\frac{\lambda}{2}xm_{3},m_{2}-\frac{\lambda}{2}xm_{4},m_{3},m_{4}\Bigr).

One can verify that this also restricts to an isomorphism
{M_{0}}\to{M_{\lambda}}
of
\Bbbk
-vector spaces.
